# Relating Animotics to Planemonics

## _Structural Eligibility vs. Physical Constraint_

### Why This Article Exists

**Animotics** and **Planemonics** answer different questions.

- **Animotics** asks:
    
    > _What kinds of animosys are physically possible?_
    
- **Planemonics** asks:
    
    > _What kinds of physical bodies exist, and under what constraints?_
    

Worldbuilders, however, inevitably ask a third question:

> _If I want a particular kind of planetary environment or animosys, what kind of world do I need to build?_

This article exists to answer that question **without collapsing the distinction between ontology and design**.

It provides **correlations, not definitions**.  
It describes **constraints, not guarantees**.  
It is **prescriptive in use**, but **descriptive in theory**.

## Directionality (Explicit and Non-Negotiable)

The relationship between Animotics and Planemonics is **one-way**.

> **Animotics defines what kind of animosys is possible.**  
> **Planemonics constrains which physical bodies can host it.**

The arrow **never points in reverse**.

- A planet’s mass, gravity, or radius **does not define** its animotic class.    
- An animotic class **does not imply** a specific planet type.    

However:

- Certain planemonic regimes **strongly correlate** with certain animotic states.    
- Other regimes **exclude them outright**.    

This article documents those correlations for **design guidance**, not classification.
## The Core Principle: Eligibility vs. Realization

Animotics describes **structural eligibility**.

Whether animosys actually appears — and how it develops — depends on **milieutics**:

- formation history    
- volatile delivery    
- stellar evolution    
- catastrophic resets    
- path dependence    

Thus:

> **Animotics answers “could.”  
> Milieutics answers “did.”**

Planemonics constrains the “could” space; it never answers the “did.”
## Typical Planemonic Correlates by Animotic Class

The following sections describe describe a **high-probability overlap phase space**, not exclusive requirements.
### Biotic Order (COH-amegic)

**Animotic definition:**  
COH-animosys is physically possible.

**Typical planemonic correlates:**

- Duromons (solid-surface bodies)    
- Mass sufficient for long-term gravity retention    
- Stable pressure–temperature regimes permitting liquid-phase solvents    
- Silicate–metal compositions    
- Atmospheres capable of cycling volatiles    

**Exclusions:**

- Airless micromons    
- Degenerate mesomons    
- Fusomons and peromons    
- Purely cryogenic or plasma-dominated bodies   

> A world may be Earth-sized and still _not_ be Biotic if its chemistry excludes COH viability.
#### High-Probability Biotic Planemonic Phase Space

The following parameter ranges describe a **typical phase space** in which **Biotic (COH-amegic) animosys is most likely to be structurally supportable**.

These values:

- are **empirical correlations**, not definitions    
- describe **high-probability overlap**, not hard boundaries    
- do **not** imply animosys will emerge    
- do **not** exclude Biotic worlds outside these ranges    

They exist solely as **design guidance**.

$$
\text{BIOTIC PLANEMONIC PHASE SPACE} := \left\{
\begin{aligned}
	&0.05 ≤ m ≤ 3 \\
	&0.35 ≤ \rho ≤ 2.18 \\
	&0.35 ≤ r(m, \rho) ≤ 2.30 \\
	&0.25 ≤ g(m, r) ≤ 3.00 \\
	&0.30 ≤ v_e(g, r) ≤ 2.63
\end{aligned}
\right.
$$

### Terric Domain (mesanthropic Biotic)

**Animotic definition:**  
COH-animosys is possible within a human-adjacent physical envelope.

**Typical planemonic correlates:**

- Planemons in the mid-mass duromon range    
- Surface gravities broadly compatible with human physiology    
- Atmospheric retention sufficient for weathering and hydrospheres    
- Internal heat budgets capable of tectonic cycling    

Terrics are the **broadest physically meaningful Biotic envelope** and allow substantial diversity in:

- climate    
- atmospheric composition    
- surface habitability
#### High-Probability Terric Planemonic Phase Space

The following parameter ranges describe a **high-probability overlap region** in planemonic phase space where **Terric (mesanthropic, COH-amegic) animosys is most often structurally supportable**.

These values:

- are **empirical correlations**, not definitions    
- describe **broad overlap**, not tight clustering    
- reflect **human-adjacent physical survivability**, not comfort or stability    
- do **not** imply animosys will emerge    
- do **not** exclude Terric worlds outside these ranges    

They exist solely as **design guidance**, indicating where solid-surface COH worlds are most likely to support long-lived geochemical cycling and remain potentially survivable for humans with technological assistance.

$$
\text{TERRIC PLANEMONIC PHASE SPACE} := \left\{ 
\begin{aligned}
&0.40 ≤ m ≤ 2.35 \\
&0.35 ≤ \rho ≤ 2.18 \\
&0.52 ≤ r(m, \rho) ≤ 1.86 \\
&0.68 ≤ g(m, \rho) ≤ 1.50 \\
&0.70 ≤ v_e(m, \rho) ≤ 1.40
\end{aligned}
\right.
$$

### Geotic Realm (maxanthropic Biotic)

**Animotic definition:**  
Stable, moderate, Earthlike environments capable of supporting long-term open-air COH-animosys.

**Typical planemonic correlates:**

- Narrower mass and density clustering than Terrics    
- Balanced gravity for both biomechanics and atmospheric stability    
- Escape velocities tuned for climate moderation    
- Long-lived tectonic and magnetic activity    

Geotics occupy the **structural core** of the Terric Domain.

They are not Earth clones — they are **Earth-mode worlds**.
#### High-Probability Geotic Planemonic Phase Space

The following parameter ranges describe a **high-probability overlap region** in planemonic phase space where **Geotic (maxanthropic, COH-amegic) animosys is most often structurally supportable**.

These values:

- are **empirical correlations**, not definitions    
- describe **statistical clustering**, not hard boundaries    
- reflect **Earth-mode physical stability**, not Earth identity    
- do **not** imply animosys will emerge    
- do **not** exclude Geotic worlds outside these ranges    

They exist solely as **design guidance**, indicating where long-term, moderate, and open-air COH environments are most likely to persist without exceptional tuning.

$$
\text{GEOTIC PLANEMONIC PHASE SPACE} := \left\{ 
\begin{aligned}
&0.60 ≤ m ≤ 1.80 \\
&0.85 ≤ \rho ≤ 1.25 \\
&0.66 ≤ r(m, \rho) ≤ 1.61 \\
&0.76 ≤ g(m, \rho) ≤ 1.40 \\
&0.71 ≤ v_e(m, \rho) ≤ 1.30
\end{aligned}
\right.
$$
### Gaean Province (melanthropic Biotic)

**Animotic definition:**  
The deep anthropic sweet spot: worlds that are freely habitable by humans without adaptation.

**Typical planemonic correlates:**

- Tight clustering around Earth-normal gravity    
- Narrow radius and density ranges    
- Exceptionally stable climates    
- Robust biosphere–atmosphere feedback loops    

> Gaeans are rare not because they are complex, but because they are **narrow**.

Every Gaean is Biotic, Terric, and Geotic — but only a small fraction of any of those classes are Gaean.
#### High-Probability Gaean Planemonic Phase Space

The following parameter ranges describe a **high-probability clustering region** in planemonic phase space where **Gaean (melanthropic, COH-amegic) animosys is most often structurally supportable**.

These values:

- are **empirical correlations**, not definitions    
- describe a **narrow clustering**, not a broad envelope    
- reflect **deep human biomechanical and ecological compatibility**, not narrative “idealness”    
- do **not** imply animosys will emerge    
- do **not** exclude Gaean worlds outside these ranges    

They exist solely as **design guidance**, indicating where freely breathable, walkable, and biomechanically effortless COH environments are most likely to persist over geologic timescales without technological mediation.
$$
\text{GAEAN PLANEMONIC PHASE SPACE} := \left\{ 
\begin{aligned}
&0.50 ≤ m ≤ 1.70 \\
&0.85 ≤ \rho ≤ 1.25 \\
&0.74 ≤ r(m, \rho) ≤ 1.33 \\
&0.90 ≤ g(m, \rho) ≤ 1.10 \\
&0.82 ≤ v_e(m, \rho) ≤ 1.21
\end{aligned}
\right.
$$

### Kybelic Domain (melamegic Biotic)

**Animotic definition:**  
COH-worlds optimized for biospheric productivity rather than human comfort.

**Typical planemonic correlates:**

- High-mass duromons    
- Elevated surface gravity    
- Dense atmospheres    
- Strong internal heat and nutrient cycling    

Kybelics are:

- morphotically Terric    
- animotically lateral    
- anthropically hostile    

They are **superhabitable for COH-animosys**, not for humans.

#### High-Probability Kybelic Planemonic Phase Space

The following parameter ranges describe a **high-probability clustering region** in planemonic phase space where **Kybelic (melamegic, COH-amegic) animosys is most often structurally supportable**.

These values:

- are **empirical correlations**, not definitions    
- describe **clustering toward high-mass, high-flux regimes**, not anthropic optima    
- reflect **maximal biospheric productivity and metabolic throughput**, not human survivability    
- do **not** imply animosys will emerge    
- do **not** exclude Kybelic worlds outside these ranges    

They exist solely as **design guidance**, indicating where COH-animosys is most likely to achieve exceptional density, diversity, and energetic richness — often at the expense of human comfort, biomechanics, or long-term habitation without intervention.


$$
\text{KYBELIC PLANEMONIC PHASE SPACE} := \left\{
\begin{aligned}
&1.00 ≤ m ≤ 2.00 \\
&0.9 ≤ \rho ≤ 1.10 \\
&1.20 ≤ r(m, \rho) ≤ 1.40 \\
&1.10 ≤ g(m, \rho) ≤ 1.40 \\
&1.15 ≤ v_e(m, \rho) ≤ 1.40
\end{aligned}
\right.
$$
### Xenotic Order (non-COH animosys)

**Animotic definition:**  
Animosys based on non-COH chemistry.

**Planemonic correlates:**

- Extremely broad    
- May include:    
    - icy worlds        
    - gas giants        
    - high-pressure interiors        
    - exotic crusts        
    - deep atmospheres        

**Key point:**

> A world may fall entirely within Terric, Geotic, or even Gaean _parameters_ and still be Xenotic if its animochemistry is alien to COH assumptions.

Physical similarity does not imply biochemical familiarity.

#### High-Probability Xenotic Planemonic Phase Space

The following parameter ranges describe a **broad, discontinuous phase space** in planemonic parameter space where **Xenotic (non-COH) animosys is most often structurally supportable**.

These values:

- are **empirical correlations**, not definitions    
- describe **multiple disjoint regimes**, not a single envelope    
- reflect **chemical and physical diversity**, not convergence    
- do **not** imply animosys will emerge    
- do **not** exclude Xenotic worlds outside these ranges    

They exist solely as **design guidance**, indicating where **non-COH animosys** may plausibly arise under a wide variety of physical conditions, many of which diverge sharply from Earth-normal assumptions.

$$
\text{XENOTIC PLANEMONIC PHASE SPACE} := \left\{
\begin{aligned}
&0.0001 ≤ m ≤ 4131 \\
&0.01 ≤ \rho ≤ 7.00 \\
&0.002 ≤ g(m, \rho) ≤ 60.00 \\
&0.02 ≤ r(m, \rho) ≤ 11.00 \\
&0.02 ≤ v_e(m, \rho) ≤ 25.00
\end{aligned}
\right.
$$

> [!attention] Please Note
> These ranges should not be read as a single continuous envelope, but as an inclusive span covering many unrelated regimes.

### Astatic Order (field-based animosys)

**Animotic definition:**  
Animosys arising from plasma, magnetic, gravitational, or quantum field coherence.

**Planemonic relevance:**  
None.

Astatics are **not planemon-hosted**.

They may:

- inhabit stellar magnetospheres    
- emerge in plasma flows    
- stabilize in gravitational saddlepoints    
- persist as non-localized field structures    

Planemonics does not constrain Astatics, because **they do not require bodies**.
#### **⟩⟩ Why There Is No Astatic Planemonic Phase Space**

Unlike Biotic, Terric, Geotic, Gaean, Kybelic, or Xenotic animosys, the **Astatic Order does not admit a planemonic phase space**.  This is not an omission.   It is a structural consequence of what Astatics are.

Astatic animosys arises from **field coherence**, not from **planemon-hosted environments**. These systems may emerge within:

- plasma flows    
- magnetic or electric field architectures    
- gravitational saddlepoints    
- relativistic regimes    
- quantum-coherent structures    

They do **not** require:

- a solid surface   
- a bounded planetary body    
- a stable mass–radius relationship    
- gravitational self-coherence at planemon scale    

As a result, traditional planemonic parameters — mass, radius, surface gravity, escape velocity, bulk density — are **not primary constraints** for Astatic animosys.

There is therefore no meaningful way to define a “high-probability planemonic phase space” for Astatics without committing a category error.

> **Astatics are constrained by field topology, flux stability, and coherence duration — not by planetary envelopes.**

Design guidance for Astatic animosys belongs to:

- plasma physics    
- magnetohydrodynamics    
- gravitational dynamics    
- quantum field theory    

—not to Planemonics.
## Design Guidance (Explicitly Non-Ontological)

This article exists so a worldbuilder can say:

- _“I want a Gaean-like environment — what should I aim for physically?”_    
- _“I want COH-animosys but not human habitability.”_    
- _“I want a familiar-looking world with alien animosys.”_    
- _“I want maximal biospheric productivity at the expense of comfort.”_    

Animotics tells you **what kind of animosys you are targeting**.  
Planemonics tells you **what physical envelopes make that plausible**.

Neither replaces the other.
## What This Article Does _Not_ Claim

- It does **not** guarantee animosys.    
- It does **not** collapse classification into parameters.    
- It does **not** imply moral or narrative outcomes.    
- It does **not** replace milieutic history.    

> **Correlation is not destiny.  
> Structure is not outcome.**

## Summary

- Animotics and Planemonics answer different questions.    
- Their relationship is **correlative, not definitional**.    
- The arrow of implication runs **Animotics → Planemonics**, never backward.    
- This article exists to support **design**, not ontology.    
- Worlds are eligible; histories decide.    

Wimby does not tell you what _will_ happen on a world.

It tells you what the universe would permit — and leaves the rest to history, chance, and choice.

> [!axiom] **The Anthropic Norm**  
> The universe sustains coherence not through extremes, but through the dominance of median, persistent states of matter and energy.  
> Rarity may inspire awe — but stability shapes reality.

---

## 🧠 Conceptual Overview

> The vast majority of matter and energy in the universe exists within a **narrow, statistically dominant band of physical states** —  from which it deviates only rarely and locally, within the current cosmological epoch.

This is the **Mediocritic Principle of State** — the idea that stability and persistence in the universe arise from the *middle modes* of physical behavior, not from the rare or the extreme.

---

## ⚖️ Two Foundational Insights

1. **Rarity is not normativity.**  
   Extreme states — from black holes to degenerate matter — may be *numerous*, but they are not **norm-defining**.  
   They are **local exceptions** to a global pattern.

2. **Normativity is modal, not mean.**  
   The Anthropic Norm does not lie at the *statistical average*,  
   but at the **modal cluster** — the zone in which **most matter and energy exist most of the time**  
   under the prevailing cosmological conditions.

---

## 🌍 Implications for Worldmaking

> [!note] **Model from the middle.**  
> In world design, the *typical* is more believable than the *spectacular*.  
> The Anthropic Norm helps define where plausibility lives.

Examples:
- The universe is filled with stars, but is mostly *not* plasma.  
  *(It was once — but that was a different epoch.)*
- Neutron stars are real, but the universe is *not* mostly neutronium.  
- Volatiles form rocky planemons,  yet the cosmos remains overwhelmingly hydrogen and helium in gaseous form.

---

## 🧩 Philosophical Corollary

The Anthropic Norm expresses the balance between **possible** and **prevalent**:
> What *can* exist defines potential;  
> What *does* exist defines probability.

In worldcraft terms:
- A believable cosmos respects **statistical gravity** — the tendency of systems to congregate near stable states.  
- The more a setting honors this mediocritic tendency, the more effortlessly it convinces.

---

> [!summary] **The Mediocritic Principle of State**  
> Stability is not found at the edges of possibility,  
> but at the center of persistence.  
> The cosmos endures because most of it behaves most of the time.

# The Astatic Order

## What the Astatic Order Is

The **Astatic Order** defines the class of animosys that does **not depend on molecular chemistry at all**.

Astatic animosys arises from:

- coherent physical fields    
- plasma dynamics    
- gravitational structures    
- quantum or symmetry-bound regimes    

Rather than carbon chains, solvents, or metabolism, Astatics persist through **field coherence, pattern stability, and energy flow**.

In short:

> **Astatic animosys is animotics without chemistry.**
## Axis Placement (Explicit and Non-Negotiable)

The Astatic Order is defined **exclusively** on the **Amegic Axis**.

- **Amegic Axis:** non-chemical (field-coherent)    
- **Anthropic Axis:** _not applicable_    
- **Morphotic Axis:** _non-duromonic_ by definition    

This matters.

The Astatic Order does **not** describe:

- planets or surfaces    
- atmospheres or oceans    
- habitability for organisms    
- environments humans could occupy    

It describes **an entirely different substrate class of animosys**.
## What “Astatic” Means (and What It Doesn’t)

**Astatic means “not grounded in matter-bound chemistry.”**

It does _not_ mean:

- immaterial    
- supernatural    
- non-physical    
- metaphysical    

Astatic animosys is:

- governed by physics    
- constrained by conservation laws    
- dependent on energy gradients    
- vulnerable to disruption    

> **Astatic is non-chemical, not non-natural.**
## Astatic as Field-Based Animosys

Where Biotic and Xenotic animosys depend on:

- molecular bonds    
- solvents and phases    
- reaction networks    

Astatic animosys depends on:

- electromagnetic structure    
- plasma circulation    
- gravitational geometry    
- quantum coherence    
- topological stability
    

Persistence arises not from reproduction in the biological sense, but from **self-maintaining dynamic patterns**.

These animosystems may:

- migrate through space    
- merge or bifurcate    
- change scale    
- persist without discrete bodies
## Relation to Other Animotic Classes

The Astatic Order is **lateral** to both Biotic and Xenotic Orders.

- **Biotic Order** — COH chemical animosys    
- **Xenotic Order** — non-COH chemical animosys    
- **Astatic Order** — non-chemical animosys    

No animosystem belongs to more than one of these orders at a time.

They are **mutually exclusive substrate classes**.
## Astatic vs. Xenotic (Critical Boundary)

The boundary between Xenotic and Astatic is **not strangeness** — it is **chemistry**.

If animosys depends on:

- molecules    
- reactions    
- solvents    
- stoichiometry    

…it is **Xenotic** (or Biotic).

If animosys depends on:

- fields    
- plasma states    
- spacetime structure    
- quantum coherence    

…it is **Astatic**.

> **If chemistry disappears and animosys persists, it is Astatic.**
## Time Matters: Astatic Is a Process, Not a Lineage

Astatic animosys is **process-based**, not genealogical.

It may:

- arise spontaneously under specific conditions    
- persist only while those conditions hold    
- vanish without trace when coherence collapses    
- re-emerge elsewhere without ancestry    

There is no assumption of:

- reproduction    
- heredity    
- evolutionary trees    

Continuity is **dynamic**, not genetic.
## Why the Astatic Order Exists in Wimby

Worldbuilders need a way to say:

> “Something alive is happening here — but no organism exists.”

The Astatic Order provides that answer without:

- forcing chemistry where it doesn’t belong    
- anthropomorphizing processes    
- relegating such phenomena to magic    

It legitimizes **non-organismic animosys** while keeping it firmly inside physics.
## Relationship to Planemonics (Explicit but Secondary)

The Astatic Order is **not defined by planetary parameters**.

Astatic animosys may arise in:

- stellar magnetospheres    
- accretion disks    
- plasma jets    
- gravitational saddle points    
- quantum-bound regions    

Planemonics may indicate **where such regimes occur**, but do not define Astatic animosys itself.

> Astatic defines _how animosys persists_.  
> Planemonics constrain _where those conditions arise_.

The arrow only points one way.
## Summary (for Fast Readers)

- **Astatic = non-chemical animosys**    
- It arises from fields, plasma, gravity, or quantum structure    
- It is neither biological nor chemical    
- It is fully physical, not metaphysical    
- It is lateral to Biotic and Xenotic Orders    
- Astatic animosys is process-based and state-dependent    

If something behaves like life but chemistry is irrelevant,  
it belongs to the **Astatic Order**.

# What the Biotic Order Is

The **Biotic Order** defines the region of possibility in which **carbon–oxygen–hydrogen (COH) chemistry** can sustain organized, persistent, energy-cycling systems recognizable as _animosys_ ("life").

It does **not** assert that animosys exists.  
It asserts that animosys is **physically possible**.

A world classified as Biotic possesses the minimum structural and environmental coherence required for:

- liquid-phase COH chemistry    
- accessible energy gradients    
- stable molecular complexity    
- long-lived metabolic cycling    

Everything else in animotics builds on this threshold.
## Axis Placement (Explicit and Non-Negotiable)

The Biotic Order is defined **exclusively** on the **[[Animotics Overview|Amegic Axis]]**.

- **Amegic Axis:** COH-amegic    
- **Anthropic Axis:** _not applied at this level_    
- **Morphotic Axis:** _not specified_    

This matters.

The Biotic Order does **not** describe:

- how comfortable a world is for humans    
- what kind of body hosts the environment    
- whether animosys actually emerges    

Those are downstream questions.
## What “Biotic” Means (and What It Doesn’t)

**Biotic means “chemically eligible for COH-animosys.”**

It does _not_ mean:

- Earthlike    
- breathable    
- temperate    
- inhabited    
- benign    

A Biotic world may be:

- lethal to humans    
- sterile due to history    
- climatically unstable    
- chemically hostile at the surface    

Yet still remain Biotic if its underlying physical regime permits COH metabolism _in principle_.

> **Biotic is a structural permission, not a biological promise.**
## The Role of Structure vs. History

The Biotic Order concerns **structure**, not outcome.

Whether animosys actually appears depends on **milieutics**:

- stellar radiation history    
- volatile delivery    
- atmospheric evolution    
- catastrophic resets    
- chemical path dependence    

Two physically indistinguishable planemons may diverge completely:

- one teeming with animosys    
- one forever sterile    

Both can still be Biotic.  This separation is intentional.  It prevents animotics from collapsing into astrobiological hindsight.
## Relation to Other Animotic Classes

The Biotic Order is the **parent class** for all COH-based animosys.

From it emerge several **anthropic strata**, which classify _human compatibility_, not substrate:

- **Terric Domain** — mesanthropic  
    COH-compatible worlds survivable by humans with technology.
    
- **Geotic Realm** — maxanthropic  
    Broadly Earthlike worlds with stable, moderate environments.
    
- **Gaean Province** — melanthropic  
    Worlds that fall into the deep anthropic sweet spot: “shirtsleeve planets.”    

In parallel—not nested—exists:

- **Kybelic Domain** — melamegic  
    COH-worlds optimized for biospheric productivity rather than human comfort.    

All of these are **Biotic**.  
None redefine what Biotic means.
## Biotic vs. Xenotic (Critical Boundary)

The Biotic Order is **chemically specific**.  It applies exclusively to:

- carbon backbones    
- oxygen-mediated energetics    
- hydrogen solvents    

Worlds whose animosys depends on fundamentally different substrates—ammonia, methane, silicates, plasmas, fields—are **not Biotic**, regardless of how “alive” they may be.

Those belong to the [[Xenotic Order (old)|**Xenotic Order**]] or **[[Astatic Order|Astatic Order]]**.

A world may be:

- physically Earth-sized    
- gravity-normal    
- atmospherically thick    

…and still **not Biotic** if its chemistry lies outside COH viability.

Substrate, not shape, draws the line.
## Time Matters: Biotic Is a State, Not a Trait

Animotic classification is **state-based**.

A single world may pass through multiple animotic states across its history:

- pre-Biotic → Biotic    
- Biotic → Terric    
- Terric → Geotic    
- Geotic → Kybelic (temporarily)    
- Biotic → non-Biotic (via loss of solvent or chemistry)    

Biotic is therefore not a permanent label.  
It is a **window of possibility** that can open and close.
## Why the Biotic Order Exists in Wimby

Worldbuilders need a way to say:

> “This planet _could_ host Earth-type animosys, even if it doesn’t.”

The Biotic Order provides that answer — cleanly, conservatively, and without narrative bias.

It is the **floor** of COH animosys, not the ceiling.

Everything above it adds specificity.  
Everything below it removes the possibility entirely.
## Relationship to Planemonics (Explicit but Secondary)

The Biotic Order is **not defined by mass, radius, gravity, or density**.  However:

- certain planemonic regimes **correlate strongly** with Biotic eligibility    
- others **exclude it outright**    

These correlations are discussed **elsewhere**, where they belong: in articles concerned with **design guidance**, not ontology.

> Biotic defines _what kind of animosys is possible_.  
> Planemonics constrains _which bodies can host it_.

The arrow only points one way.
## Summary (for Fast Readers)

- **Biotic = COH-animosys is physically possible**    
- It is an **amegic classification**, not an anthropic one    
- It says nothing about comfort, habitability, or inhabitants    
- It is the parent class of Terric, Geotic, Gaean, and Kybelic worlds    
- It is distinct from Xenotic and Astatic animosys    
- It is state-based, historically contingent, and deliberately conservative    

If a world is not Biotic, **COH-animosys cannot arise there at all**.  
If it _is_ Biotic, everything else is still up for grabs.

#coh #animotics #habitability# The Canonical Animotics Tree

**The Animotics Tree** is the canonical structural schema of the Worldmaking Basics animotics framework.

It enumerates representative **Orders, Domains, Realms, and Provinces** by which animosys-supporting environments are classified, and encodes their **nesting and lateral relationships**.

This document is a **reference artifact**, not an explanatory essay.  Conceptual definitions, phenomenology, and design guidance are provided in the linked articles for each category.

The Tree is **illustrative rather than exhaustive**: inclusion denotes ontological recognition, not completeness, prevalence, or developmental priority.

## Tree Listing

- Biotic Order (minanthropic)

# The Distance Modulus

And _this_ is where the distances to the stars comes back into the story.  **Apparent magnitude** and **Absolute Magnitude** are related by a relationship called _the distance modulus_, mathematically expressed as:

$$
m - M = 5\;log_{10}(d) - 5
$$

Where $d$ is the distance to the star in _parsecs_.

So, a star's **Absolute magnitude** is how bright the star would appear to us if it were exactly $10$ parsecs away.  This is less straighforward than the simple inverse-square law that governs apparent brightness for _local_ observers, and might not really be that useful, unless you're wanting to know how bright your star would appear to someone looking at it from a significant distance away from your star system.
### Magnitude and Luminosity

The general equation relating **absolute magnitude** ($M$) to stellar luminosity in stellar units is:

$$
M = M\!⊙ - 2.5\; log_{10}(L)
$$

Where:
- $L$ = the star's luminosity in solar units.

Plugging in the Sun's absolute magnitude value $M=4.83$:

$$
M = 4.83 - 2.5\; log_{10}(L)
$$

… and rearranging:

$$
4.83 = -2.5\;log_{10}(C)
$$

… and solving for C:

$$
C = 10^{-\frac{4.83}{2.5}} \approx 0.01169
$$

… and rearranging again:

$$
M = -2.5\;log_{10}(0.01169L)
$$

Where:

- $L$ = the luminosity of the star in solar units.

… the inverse of which is:

$$
L = 85.5432 \times 2.512^{-M}
$$

Where:

- $M$ = the star's absolute magnitude.

This gives us a rough approximation of any star's absolute magnitude and we can use the **distance modulus** to calculate an apparent magnitude from that.

# The Euclidean Algorithm

The Euclidean Algorithm provides an elegant and efficient (if somewhat involved) way to compute the greatest common divisor (GCD) of two integers.  This is especially useful for simplifying ratios or finding integer relationships when work with synodic systems

## The Algorithm
The algorithm itself is deceptively short and simple:
1. Start with two integers $a$ and $b$, such that $a>b$.
2. Divide $a$ by $b$, and note the *remainder*, $r$.
	- $r = a\; MOD\; b$
3. Replace $a$ with $b$ and $b$ with $r$.
4. Repeat steps 2 - 3 until $f = 0$.

The last *non-zero remainder* is the greatest common divisor between $a$ and $b$.

### Example

$$
\begin{aligned}
a = 2436 \qquad b = 1172 \\
r = a\;mod\;b = 92 \\[1em]
a = 1172 \qquad b = 92 \\
r = a\;mod\;b = 68 \\[1em]
a = 92 \qquad b = 68 \\
r = a\;mod\;b = 24 \\[1em]
a = 68 \qquad b = 24 \\
r = a\;mod\;b = 20 \\[1em]
a = 24 \qquad b = 20 \\
r = a\;mod\;b = 4\; ✓ \\[1em]
a = 20 \qquad b = 4 \\
⟶ r = a\;mod\;b = 0 \\
\end{aligned}
$$

If you don't have a tool that directly calculates modulos, $r$ can manually be calculated by:

$$
r = a - \left( b \times \left\lfloor \dfrac{a}{b} \right\rfloor \right)
$$

Here is an admittedly clunky longhand version for clarity

$$
\begin{aligned}
2 &\quad (Quotient)\\[-0.2ex]
1172)\,\overline{2436} &\quad (Dividend)\\[-0.4ex]
\underline{-2344} &\quad (Subtracted: 2\times1172)\\[-0.3ex]
92 &\leftarrow (Remainder)
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
12 &\quad (Quotient)\\[-0.2ex]
92)\,\overline{1172} &\quad (Dividend)\\[-0.4ex]
\underline{-1104} &\quad (Subtracted: 12\times92)\\[-0.3ex]
68 &\leftarrow (Remainder)
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
1 &\quad (Quotient)\\[-0.2ex]
68)\,\overline{92} &\quad (Dividend)\\[-0.4ex]
\underline{-68} &\quad (Subtracted: 1\times68)\\[-0.3ex]
24 &\leftarrow (Remainder)
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
2 &\quad (Quotient)\\[-0.2ex]
24)\,\overline{68} &\quad (Dividend)\\[-0.4ex]
\underline{-48} &\quad (Subtracted: 2\times24)\\[-0.3ex]
20 &\leftarrow (Remainder)
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
1 &\quad (Quotient)\\[-0.2ex]
20)\,\overline{24} &\quad (Dividend)\\[-0.4ex]
\underline{-20} &\quad (Subtracted: 1\times20)\\[-0.3ex]
\mathbf{4} &\;\boldsymbol{\checkmark}\leftarrow (Remainder)
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\begin{aligned}
5 &\quad (Quotient)\\[-0.2ex]
4)\,\overline{20} &\quad (Dividend)\\[-0.4ex]
\underline{-20} &\quad (Subtracted: 5\times4)\\[-0.3ex]
0 &\leftarrow (Remainder)
\end{aligned}
$$

$$
\text{Since this remainder is } 0, \text{ the previous remainder is the GCD:}\\[0.5ex]
\therefore\;\gcd(2436,1172)=4
$$

🔝 [[Standard Mathematics]]

# The Gaean Province

## What the Gaean Province Is

The **Gaean Province** defines the class of **melanthropic** worlds within the **Geotic Realm**:  
COH-amegic environments whose physical structure lies at the **center of human physiological, biomechanical, and ecological tolerance**.

Where Geotic worlds are broadly habitable, **Gaean worlds are natively comfortable**.

A Gaean world supports:

- open-air human life without assistance    
- effortless locomotion and respiration    
- long-term ecological stability    
- intuitive environmental interaction    

In short:

> **Gaean worlds are those where humans belong without modification.**
## Axis Placement (Explicit and Non-Negotiable)

The Gaean Province is defined by the **intersection** of two axes:

- **Amegic Axis:** COH-amegic (inherited from Biotic)    
- **Anthropic Axis:** melanthropic    

It is a **refinement**, not a redefinition, of the Geotic Realm.

- **Morphotic Axis:** typically duromonic, but not defined here    

This matters.  The Gaean Province does **not** describe:

- cultural familiarity    
- aesthetic similarity to Earth    
- biological inevitability    
- narrative importance    

It describes **anthropic optimality**.
## What “Gaean” Means (and What It Doesn’t)

**Gaean means “anthropically optimal.”**  It does _not_ mean:

- identical to Earth    
- ecologically static    
- biologically conservative    
- free of danger    

A Gaean world may be:

- alien in flora and fauna    
- climatically varied    
- tectonically active    
- culturally unfamiliar    

Yet still be Gaean if its **baseline physical regime** aligns closely with human norms.

> **Gaean is about ease, not sameness.**
## The Anthropically Effortless World

Within the Geotic Realm, the Gaean Province occupies the **deepest anthropic sweet spot**:  
the region where planetary structure minimizes physiological strain and environmental mediation.

This is the zone where:

- breathing is unconscious    
- walking feels natural    
- gravity disappears from awareness    
- weather is challenging but not hostile    
- ecosystems feel intuitively legible    

Gaean worlds do not merely permit human life.  
They **welcome it**.
## Relation to Other Animotic Classes

The Gaean Province is a **subset** of the Geotic Realm and the **most restrictive anthropic class** in the Biotic Order.

- **Terric Domain** — human survival possible    
- **Geotic Realm** — human habitation viable    
- **Gaean Province** — human comfort native    

All Gaeans are Geotic.  
Most Geotics are not Gaean.

This asymmetry is intentional and unavoidable.
## Rarity Is the Point

The Gaean Province is **narrow by definition**.

Only a fraction of Geotic worlds fall deeply enough into the anthropic optimum to qualify as Gaean. This is not a failure of generosity; it is a reflection of biological specificity.

Human physiology is:

- gravity-sensitive    
- pressure-sensitive    
- chemically constrained    
- evolutionarily contingent    

The fact that Gaeans are rare is not surprising.  
It is expected.

> **A world finely tuned to one lineage should be uncommon in a vast cosmos.**
## Time Matters: Gaean Is a Moment, Not a Birthright

Like all animotic classifications, Gaean is **state-based**.

A world may:

- pass briefly through the Gaean Province    
- remain Gaean for billions of years    
- drift out due to climatic or tectonic change    
- never enter it at all    

Gaean status reflects **current anthropic alignment**, not destiny or narrative privilege.
## Why the Gaean Province Exists in Wimby

Worldbuilders need a way to say:

> “This planet feels like home.”

The Gaean Province provides that answer without:

- declaring Earth uniqueness    
- collapsing comfort into habitability    
- pretending humans are universal    

It names the **rare convergence** of conditions under which human life is not merely possible, but **effortless**.
## Relationship to Planemonics (Explicit but Secondary)

The Gaean Province is **not defined by equations**.

Certain combinations of:

- mass    
- gravity    
- density    
- escape velocity    

**correlate strongly** with anthropic ease, while others impose subtle but persistent strain. These correlations are treated as **design guidance**, not definitional boundaries.

> Gaean defines _how a world feels to humans_.  
> Planemonics constrains _where that feeling is likely to occur_.

The arrow only points one way.

## Summary (for Fast Readers)

- **Gaean = melanthropic Geotic worlds**    
- Humans can live openly and effortlessly    
- Gaean does not imply Earth identity    
- It is the deepest anthropic sweet spot    
- All Gaeans are Geotic; most Geotics are not Gaean    
- Gaean is state-based and historically contingent    

If a world is Gaean, humans do not have to adapt.  
They simply arrive.

# The Geotic Realm

## What the Geotic Realm Is

The **Geotic Realm** defines the class of **maxanthropic** worlds within the **Terric Domain**:  
COH-amegic environments whose physical structure **naturally supports stable, long-term human habitation** without extensive technological mediation.

Geotic worlds are not required to be perfect Earth analogs.  
They are required to be **structurally forgiving**.

A Geotic world supports:

- COH-animosys robustly and persistently    
- open-air human habitation with minimal intervention    
- long-term climatic and geochemical stability    

In short:

> **Geotic worlds are those where humans can live, not merely survive.**
## Axis Placement (Explicit and Non-Negotiable)

The Geotic Realm is defined by the **intersection** of two axes:

- **Amegic Axis:** COH-amegic (inherited from Biotic)    
- **Anthropic Axis:** maxanthropic    

It further refines, but does not replace, the Terric Domain.

- **Morphotic Axis:** typically duromonic, but not defined here    

This matters.  The Geotic Realm does **not** describe:

- cultural suitability   
- aesthetic similarity to Earth    
- biological inevitability    
- narrative importance    

Those are downstream concerns.
## What “Geotic” Means (and What It Doesn’t)

**Geotic means “naturally habitable for humans.”** It does _not_ mean:

- identical to Earth    
- climatically uniform    
- ecologically mature    
- free of hazards    

A Geotic world may be:

- colder or warmer than Earth    
- wetter or drier    
- biologically young    
- sparsely populated    

Yet still be Geotic if its **baseline physical regime** supports human life without extraordinary engineering.

> **Geotic is about habitability, not perfection.**
## Geotic as the Earthlike Core of Terric Space

Within the Terric Domain, the Geotic Realm occupies the **structural core**:  
the region where planetary mass, gravity, atmosphere, and heat flow **align naturally** with human physiology and COH-animosys.

This is the zone where:

- breathable atmospheres are stable    
- hydrospheres persist over geological time    
- plate tectonics and magnetic shielding are common    
- climates self-regulate within tolerable bounds    

Geotic worlds are therefore **evolution-friendly**, not merely survivable.
## Relation to Other Animotic Classes

The Geotic Realm is a **subset** of the Terric Domain and a **superset** of the Gaean Province.

- **Terric Domain** — human survival possible    
- **Geotic Realm** — human habitation viable    
- **Gaean Province** — human comfort native    

All Gaeans are Geotic.  
Most Geotics are not Gaean.

This asymmetry is deliberate.
## Geotic vs. Gaean (Critical Distinction)

The Geotic Realm is defined by **structural habitability**.  
The **Gaean Province** is defined by **anthropic optimality**.

Geotic worlds:

- support human life naturally    
- tolerate variation in gravity, climate, and chemistry    
- permit ecological diversity    

Gaean worlds:

- cluster tightly around Earth-normal parameters    
- minimize physiological and environmental stress    
- occupy the deepest anthropic sweet spot    

Gaeans are rare by construction.  
Geotics are rare by circumstance.
## Time Matters: Geotic Is a State, Not a Guarantee

Like all animotic classifications, Geotic is **state-based**.

A world may:

- enter the Geotic Realm as atmospheres stabilize    
- leave it as climates drift or gravity changes    
- pass through Geotic briefly on the way to Gaean    
- degrade back to Terric through stellar or geological change    

Geotic status reflects **present structure**, not destiny.
## Why the Geotic Realm Exists in Wimby

Worldbuilders need a way to say:

> “Humans can live here, and the planet will mostly cooperate.”

The Geotic Realm provides that answer without:

- assuming Earth identity    
- collapsing habitability into comfort    
- treating humans as cosmically typical    

It marks the **true corridor of habitability** within the Biotic Order:  
broad enough for diversity, narrow enough to mean something.
## Relationship to Planemonics (Explicit but Secondary)

The Geotic Realm is **not defined by numerical thresholds**.

Certain ranges of:

- mass    
- gravity    
- density    
- escape velocity    

**correlate strongly** with Geotic habitability, while others exclude it outright. These correlations are treated as **design guidance**, not classification criteria.

> Geotic defines _natural habitability_.  
> Planemonics constrains _where that habitability is likely_.

The arrow only points one way.
## Summary (for Fast Readers)

- **Geotic = maxanthropic Terric worlds**    
- COH-animosys thrives, and humans can live openly    
- Geotic does not imply Earth identity or perfection    
- It is the Earthlike core of Terric space    
- All Gaeans are Geotic; most Geotics are not Gaean    
- Geotic is state-based and historically contingent    

If a world is Geotic, human life is naturally supported.  
How _pleasant_ that life is depends on whether it is also Gaean.

> [!quote]
> > “The universe is not only stranger than we imagine;
> > it is stranger than we are able to imagine.”
> > — J. B. S. Haldane (paraphrased for modern audiences)
> > 

> > Wimby adopts this as a principle.
> > 
> > We map the parts we can understand, we scaffold the parts we can use, and we leave the rest open to invention.
> > 
> > Worldmaking thrives not by pretending the cosmos is simple, but by navigating what can be known — and playing generously with the rest.

---
# 🧩 GEWE — Good Enough for Worldmaking Efforts

## 🧩 Definition  
**GEWE** stands for **Good Enough for Worldmaking Efforts.**

It marks the **pragmatic threshold of adequacy** — the moment when the *needs of the story, culture, drama, or play experience* legitimately outweigh strict technical precision.

Think of it as the creator’s quiet permission slip:

> *Mundus meum est* — “It’s **my** world.”

GEWE exists for the times when a world works better if you bend truth a little rather than break the story a lot.

It’s not sloppiness.  
It’s **intentional deviation in service of coherence.**

---

# 🌍 Examples Across Crafting and Building

## **Astronomical (Logos → Mythos)**

- The orbital calculation says the year is 319.789 days.  
  The story says:  
  **Call it 320. Move on.**

- A Mars-sized moon shouldn’t survive that close to a gas giant.  
  But your sacred solstice rite needs it looming over the ocean.  
  **Drama wins.**

## **Biological**

- Real-world mules are sterile.  
  But Book Three depends on a prize-winning mule siring offspring.  
  **GEWE smiles.**

- The apex predator should gestate for months.  
  Your plot needs “eggs hatch overnight.”  
  **Approved.**

## **Cultural / Sociological**

- A medieval village couldn’t support three specialized guilds.  
  But narratively, it’s a cultural crossroads.  
  **Mythos says yes.**

- The floating sky-city is economically ridiculous.  
  But symbolically essential.  
  **GEWE keeps it aloft.**

## **Magical / Metaphysical**

- Your magic system has rules.  
  The gods have exceptions.  
  **GEWE considers this a feature.**

- The reservoir is finite…  
  …except when the protagonist truly needs one more spell.  
  **This once, she reaches deeper.**

## **Ecological / Environmental**

- The planet should be frozen at that albedo and semi-major axis.  
  But you need a thriving temperate forest.  
  **“Unmodeled greenhouse factor. Done.”**

- This valley has implausible biodiversity.  
  **It’s a refugium.** No further questions.

## **Physics / Technology**

- The power budget says no simultaneous life support *and* full weapons.  
  The set-piece battle says otherwise.  
  **Experimental capacitor bank. Next problem.**

- FTL messages violate causality?  
  **Only if you let them.**

---

# 🎭 Core Principle

“So,” you may be asking, “why have WMB at all if I can ignore it?”

Because WMB exists to give you **two legitimate creative paths**:

1. **Honor the physics and let the story adapt**, or  
2. **Ignore the physics intelligently when the story demands it.**

GEWE is not about handwaving.  
It’s about choosing **coherence of experience** over **coherence of calculation** when the two collide.

When story, character, emotion, theme, symbolism, cadence, or gameplay would suffer under strict precision, GEWE steps forward and says:

> **“Bend the rule, not the world.”**

Let [[Cascading Rounding Notation]] handle the numeric honesty.  
Let **GEWE** protect the narrative truth.

But remember that though you are the first-and-final arbiter, you still have a responsibility to your milieu.  Check out [[To GEWE or Not To GEWE]] for some advice.


🔝 [[01. What is WMB?]]

## Hill Sphere
In any multi-body system, the **Hill sphere** defines the region around a smaller body within which its own gravity dominates over the tidal influence of a larger primaron.  Within this sphere, satellites, rings, or retained debris can maintain long-term stable orbits around the smaller body.

Conceptually, the Hill sphere is the **circum-orbital analog** of the Roche lobe:  where the Roche lobe marks the boundary between *two comparable masses*, the Hill sphere marks the boundary between a **primaron** and a **much smaller secondron**.

### Classical Formulation
For a secondron body of mass $M_2$ orbiting a much larger primaron of mass $M_1$ at semi-major axis $\alpha$, the radius of its Hill sphere is approximated by:

$$
H_r = \alpha\;
       \sqrt[3]{
         \frac{M_2}{3M_1}
       }
$$
Where:
- $H_r$ = Hill-sphere radius (the limit of stable satellite orbits)  
- $\alpha$ = orbital semi-major axis of the secondron about the primaron  
- $M_2$ = mass of the secondron (planet, moon, etc.)  
- $M_1$ = mass of the primaron (star, planet, etc.)

### Interpretation
- Inside $H_r$, the secondron’s gravity dominates; small bodies can orbit it stably.  
- Near or beyond $H_r$, tidal forces from the primaron destabilize those orbits.  
- The Hill sphere thus defines the **outer boundary of a planet’s satellite system** or, in stellar terms, the **limit of gravitational capture**.

### Rule of Thumb
- Long-term stable satellite orbits typically require  

$$
  r_{\text{sat}} \lesssim 0.5\,H_r
$$

  — i.e., within roughly half the Hill radius.

- For planets in circular orbits, the expression is accurate to within a few percent.
- For eccentric orbits, replace $\alpha$ with the **periapsis distance**, $\alpha(1 - e)$, to account for stronger tidal effects at closest approach.

### Comparison with Roche Lobe
- **Roche Lobe**
  - Two bodies of comparable mass (binaries).
  - Equipotential surface where gravitational and centrifugal forces balance.
  - Teardrop-shaped, asymmetric about $L_1$.
- **Hill Sphere**
  - Large primaron with small secondron (planet-moon; star-planet).
  - Distance from secondron where its gravitational influence equals the primaron's tidal force.
  - Roughly spherical for $m \ll M$.

 **Analogy:** The Roche lobe is the *shared frontier* between equals; the Hill sphere is the *personal domain* of a subordinate.

### Example — Earth’s Hill Sphere

For Earth ($M_2 = 1\,M_\oplus$) orbiting the Sun ($M_1 = 1\,M_\odot$) at $\alpha = 1\,\text{AU}$:

$$
H_r = 1\,\text{AU}
       \left(
         \frac{1/333{,}000}{3}
       \right)^{\!\tfrac{1}{3}}
     \approx 0.01\,\text{AU} \approx 1.5\times10^6\,\text{km}
$$

That comfortably encloses the Moon’s orbit ($≈ 384{,}000$ km), confirming its long-term stability.

# The Main Sequence
 ![[Reverse H-R Diagram-Expanded.png]]
> [!attention]
> Stellar radii in this illustration are indicative only. The diagram prioritizes stellar regimes and parameter relationships over geometric scale.
> 
> This diagram is horizontally reversed from traditional H-R diagrams, which have hotter temperatures to the left and cooler temperatures to the right.  There are historical reasons for the “backward” format of traditional H–R diagrams, but for Wimby purposes, a diagram that follows intuitive left–right and up–down conventions is more useful.  This diagram contains the same information as a standard H–R diagram, presented in a more intuitive orientation.

In stellar astronomy, the **Main Sequence** refers to the long-lived phase of a star’s life during which it fuses hydrogen into helium in its core. During this phase, a star maintains hydrostatic equilibrium, and its luminosity, surface temperature, radius, and lifetime are tightly correlated.

Most stars spend the majority of their lifetimes on the Main Sequence, and many of the [[Stars and Spectral Classes|empirical relations]] used in stellar astrophysics are derived from stars in this phase.

The concept emerged alongside the development of the [Hertzsprung–Russell diagram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hertzsprung–Russell_diagram) (H-R diagram) in the early 20th century, as astronomers recognized that stars cluster along a distinct band when plotted by luminosity and temperature.

In **Wimby stellamonics**, the Main Sequence is treated not merely as a classification, but as a **stable stellar regime** — the domain in which long-term orbital structure, energy availability, and habitability can be meaningfully discussed.  This is the regime in which the [[Stellamon Equations of State|power-law relations]] used throughout stellamonics are valid to first order.

Unless otherwise stated, Wimby stellamonic relations assume a star lies on the Main Sequence. This assumption is not universal, but it is explicit.

## Pre- and Post-Main Sequence

A pre-[Main-Sequence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_sequence) star is:

- a collapsing protostar    
- powered primarily by **gravitational contraction**, not fusion    
- still settling toward hydrostatic and thermal equilibrium

Pre-Main-Sequence stars are short-lived as a phase (they evolve into Main Sequence stars rapidly), unstable, and poor hosts for long-term planetary habitability.

This phase ends when:

- the core becomes hot and dense enough    
- **sustained hydrogen fusion** begins    
- the star enters the Main Sequence

> [!important]
> There is no category of hydrogen-fusing star that skips the Main Sequence because it’s too massive.

[UY Scuti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UY_Scuti) ($909$ times the Sun's radius and $124,000$ times its luminosity), for instance, began its life as a very massive Main Sequence star.  It has since evolved _off_ the Main Sequence into a late-stage supergiant.

- it started as a very massive [[Stars and Spectral Classes|O- or early B-type]] star    
- it burned hydrogen in its core (Main Sequence phase)    
- it exhausted that hydrogen very quickly (a few million years)
- it expanded enormously as fusion moved into shells    
- it is now in a **short, unstable, late evolutionary phase**

UY Scuti is an extreme example of a post-Main Sequence star.

UY Scuti also illustrates a common source of confusion in stellar classification — and a frequent embarrassment in worldmaking.  Spectral class describes a star’s [**surface temperature**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effective_temperature) (or *effective temperature*, sometimes denoted $T_{eff}$), _not_ its size, mass, luminosity, **or** evolutionary state.  While Main Sequence stars of a given spectral class tend to share broadly similar properties, this correspondence breaks down completely once a star leaves the Main Sequence.

UY Scuti is [[Stars and Spectral Classes|Spectral Class M]] (M2 – M4); however, it is not an "M-dwarf", but a hypergiant of [[Stellar Luminosity Classes|luminosity class 0]].  The reason it shares a spectral class with small red stars like [Proxima Centauri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proxima_Centauri) (M5.5) is that spectral classes and spectral types are based on a star's surface (effective) temperature.  UY Scuti has a surface temperature of $3500$ K compared to Proxima Centauri's $2992$ K, so they are similar in temperature and color, but that is where any similarities end.  This is why luminosity classes were invented ([[Stellar Luminosity Classes]]).

So, a post–Main Sequence star may share a spectral class with the Sun or other familiar stars while differing from them by orders of magnitude in radius, luminosity, and energy output. For this reason, stellamonics treats spectral class as **necessary but not sufficient** for understanding a star’s structural and habitability implications.

## Post–Main Sequence Stars

Once a star exhausts the hydrogen fuel in its core, it leaves the Main Sequence. From this point onward, the tight parameter coupling that characterizes Main Sequence stars begins to break down.

Luminosity, radius, surface temperature, and energy output may change rapidly or non-monotonically, often on timescales short compared to planetary climate or biological evolution.

For this reason, post–Main Sequence stars fall outside the default assumptions of stellamonics. They are not [[Habitable Zones of Giant Stars|ignored]], but they are treated as special cases rather than baseline environments.

# Conclusion
Wimby stellamonics treats spectral class as [**necessary but not sufficient**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Necessity_and_sufficiency) for understanding a star’s structural and habitability implications.

# The Naked-Eye Limit of Object Visibility

Even without a telescope, the human eye is a sensitive instrument.  
Under a perfectly dark, moonless sky far from city lights, a person with average eyesight can see stars down to about **magnitude 6.0** — roughly **one-millionth** as bright as the faintest stars that remain visible from a town.

But brightness isn’t everything. A planet, asteroid, or moonlet that *reflects* light rather than producing its own must also be **large enough** and **close enough** for its surface area to gather and scatter enough starlight toward the observer.

In practice:

| Sky Condition     | Naked-Eye Limit | Example                                    |
| :---------------- | :-------------- | :----------------------------------------- |
| City sky          | ≈ mag 3         | Only the brightest dozen stars and planets |
| Suburban sky      | ≈ mag 4.5       | Dozens of stars; Milky Way faintly hinted  |
| Rural dark site   | ≈ mag 6         | Several thousand stars visible             |
| High desert / sea | ≈ mag 6.5 – 7   | Milky Way casts a faint shadow             |

The *naked-eye limit* defines the practical boundary between **visible** and **imagined** objects in a sky.

When worldbuilding, anything dimmer than about **mag 6** should be considered invisible to ordinary people unless aided by magic, technology, or narrative emphasis.

# The Ontic Hierarchy: Ontic Regimes

**Ontic Regimes** describe the dominant cohesive forces that define how matter and energy maintain unity.  
> They trace the progression from the smallest bound structures to the largest self-coherent bodies — **from nuclei to stars and beyond.**

| **Regime**                               | **Dominant Binding Forces**              | **Scale**                                           | **Examples**                             |
| :--------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------------------- |
| Nuclear Regime                           | Strong and Weak Nuclear                  | Subatomic and Nuclear                               | neutronium, fusion cores                 |
| **Microphysical Regime**                 | Electroweak<br>(atomic/molecular EM)<br> | Atomics, Ions, <br>Molecular and Crystalline matter | atoms, elements, compounds               |
| **Macrophysical Regime**<br>(*Mononics*) | Electrochemical and Gravitic             | Planetary, degenerate, and stellar bodies           | duromons, mesomons, fusomons, peromons   |
| **Field Regime**                         | Energetic or spacetime curvature fields  | Exotic, collapse-bound, or post-stellar systems     | black holes, cryptomons, hyperstellamons |

Each regime represents a distinct **mode of coherence**, determined by the dominant physical interaction at its scale.  
Together they form the backbone of **Ontic hierarchy**, linking the smallest bound particle to the largest self-coherent body.

## From Ontic Regimes to Mononics

For WMB purposes, the **Macrophysical Regime** is where our real work begins.  
The Nuclear, Microphysical, and Field regimes matter — they set the stage, establish constraints, and occasionally impress themselves upon the macrocosm — but they are not where **worldcrafters** operate day-to-day.

> **Mononics** is the domain of worldcrafters:  
> the study of _self-coherent bodies_ — moons, planets, mesomons, stellamons, and all other macro-objects whose behavior is shaped by bulk cohesion or gravity.

This is the level at which crafted worlds acquire their physical identity and begin expressing the physical characteristics that will later shape and support climates, life, culture, and story.

# Ontic Mass Continuum
## Introduction

**Mass intervals describe scale, but not class.**  
A monon’s [[02. The Ontic Mass Continuum|_Ontic Class_]] (duromon, mesomon, fusomon, peromon) depends on its **internal physics** — whether cohesion is electrochemical, gravitational, degenerate-electron, or fusional — not solely the amount of mass it contains.

Mass domains are therefore **diagnostic**, not **determinative**:  
they tell you _how big something is_, not _what kind of object it is_.

The following conventions establish how **Worldmaking Basics (WMB)** names and interprets mass intervals relative to Earth’s mass ($t$). These definitions ensure that both scientific notation and symbolic terminology remain intuitive across a vast range of scales.

### Ontic Mass Domains
$$
\begin{array}{l c r r r l}
\textbf{Intervals} &
\textbf{Abbr.} &
\textbf{Min. Mass} > &
\textbf{Max. Mass} \leq &
\textbf{Power} \\[3pt]
\hline
\text{deniterran}  & \text{dnt} & 0.0000000001 & 0.000000001   & 10^{-10} \\
\text{nanoterran}  & \text{nnt}  & 0.000000001  & 0.00000001    & 10^{-9}  \\
\text{oktiterran}  & \text{okt} & 0.00000001   & 0.0000001     & 10^{-8}  \\
\text{septiterran} & \text{spt} & 0.0000001    & 0.000001      & 10^{-7}  \\
\text{microterran} & \text{μt}     & 0.000001     & 0.00001       & 10^{-6}  \\
\text{pentiterran} & \text{pnt} & 0.00001      & 0.0001        & 10^{-5}  \\
\text{demiterran}  & \text{dmt} & 0.0001       & 0.001         & 10^{-4}  \\
\text{milliterran} & \text{mt}  & 0.001        & 0.01          & 10^{-3}  \\
\text{centiterran} & \text{ct}  & 0.01         & 0.1           & 10^{-2}  \\
\text{deciterran}  & \text{dt}  & 0.1          & 1             & 10^{-1}  \\
\text{terran}      & \text{t}  & 1            & 10            & 10^{0}   \\
\text{dekaterran}  & \text{dat} & 10           & 100           & 10^{1}   \\
\text{hectoterran} & \text{ht}  & 100          & 1000          & 10^{2}   \\
\text{kiloterran}  & \text{kt}  & 1000         & 10000         & 10^{3}   \\
\text{myriaterran} & \text{Mrt} & 10000        & 100000        & 10^{4}   \\
\text{quintaterran}  & \text{Qnt} & 100000       & 1000000       & 10^{5}   \\
\text{megaterran}  & \text{Mt} & 1000000      & 10000000      & 10^{6}   \\
\text{heptaterran} & \text{Hpt} & 10000000     & 100000000     & 10^{7}   \\
\text{octaterran}  & \text{Oct} & 100000000    & 1000000000    & 10^{8}   \\
\text{gigaterran}  & \text{Gt} & 1000000000   & 10000000000   & 10^{9}   \\
\text{denaterran}  & \text{Ddt} & 10000000000  & 100000000000  & 10^{10}  \\
\text{ondaterran}  & \text{Ont} & 100000000000 & 1000000000000 & 10^{11}  \\
\text{teraterran}  & \text{Tt}  & 1000000000000 & 10000000000000 & 10^{12} \\
\text{triskaterran}& \text{Trt} & 10000000000000 & 100000000000000 & 10^{13} \\
\text{quadraterran}& \text{Qdt} & 100000000000000 & 1000000000000000 & 10^{14} \\
\text{petaterran}  & \text{Pt} & 1000000000000000 & 10000000000000000 & 10^{15} \\
\text{sexaterran}  & \text{Sxt} & 10000000000000000 & 100000000000000000 & 10^{16} \\
\text{septaterran} & \text{Stt} & 100000000000000000 & 1000000000000000000 & 10^{17} \\
\text{exaterran}   & \text{Et} & 1000000000000000000 & 10000000000000000000 & 10^{18} \\
\end{array}
$$

- Where official SI prefixes exist (e.g., *micro-*, *milli-*, etc.), **WMB** uses them directly.
- For intermediate powers without SI prefixes, **WMB** adopts consistent [[Wimby Extension of the SI Prefix System|neologisms]] based on the exponent’s magnitude:
	- Exponents **below zero** use an *-i* ending (unless an SI prefix already exists, e.g., *micro-*, not *micri-*).  
	-  Exponents **above zero** use an *-a* ending (unless an SI prefix already exists, e.g., *kilo-*, not *kila-*).
- While SI defines prefixes beyond *exa-* ([Metric prefix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metric_prefix)), **WMB** rarely uses them in Ontic contexts.  
   - Even the observable universe spans only ≈ $46.5$ **giga**light-years (Gly) in radius — roughly three orders of magnitude below **teraterran** relevance.  
- The **negative intervals** are symbolically essential: many moons, asteroids, and micro-monons fall far below $1t$ in mass.  
   - It is often **clearer** to say  
	   - “The mass is $1$ microterran $(μt)$”  than  “The body is $0.000001t$.”  
- For practical purposes, **WMB** defines the upper bound of each mass interval as **one demiterran** ($10^{-5};\;0.00001t$) below the next power of ten.  
   - Thus, the *terran* mass interval **technically spans** 
     $1.0\,t ≤ M < 9.99999\,t$.  
   - Any value exceeding that threshold — even slightly — **rounds up** to the next symbolic interval.  
     - **Example:**
        $9.999 99 1\,t → 10.0\.t$, assigning the body to the **dekaterran** interval.  
- The **deniterran** marks the lower limit of the scale; bodies smaller than this cannot achieve hydrostatic equilibrium.  
- The **exaterran** marks the upper limit, approximating the realistic mass ceiling for Ultra-massive Black Holes (UMBHs), about $10^{12}\,M\,s$ ([Supermassive_black_hole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermassive_black_hole#Description)).
## The Microterran Subscale

Because many **monons** have masses best expressed as a **millionth of a terran**, WMB defines an optional *microterran subscale* ($μt$) for intuitive symbolic modeling.

This subscale maps cleanly to the standard symbolic intervals and absolute powers of ten.

$$
\begin{array}{l r r l r r r}
\textbf{$\mu$terran Scale} &
\textbf{Min. Mass} &
\textbf{Max. Mass} &
\textbf{Std. Scale} &
\textbf{Power} \\[3pt]
\hline\hline
\text{demimicro (dmμt)}  & 0.0001 & 0.001  & \text{deniterran}  & 10^{-10} \\
\text{millimicro (mμt)}  & 0.001  & 0.01   & \text{nanoterran}  & 10^{-9}  \\
\text{centimicro (cμt)}  & 0.01   & 0.1    & \text{oktiterran}  & 10^{-8}  \\
\text{decimicro (dμt)}   & 0.1    & 1      & \text{septiterran} & 10^{-7}  \\
\text{microterran (μt)}  & 1      & 10     & \text{microterran} & 10^{-6}  \\
\text{dekamicro (daμt)}  & 10     & 100    & \text{pentiterran} & 10^{-5}  \\
\text{hectomicro (hμt)}  & 100    & 1000   & \text{demiterran}  & 10^{-4}  \\
\text{kilomicro (kμt)}   & 1000   & 10000  & \text{milliterran} & 10^{-3}  \\
\text{myriamicro (Mrμt)} & 10000  & 100000 & \text{centiterran} & 10^{-2}  \\
\text{quintamicro (Qnμt)}& 100000 & 1000000& \text{deciterran}  & 10^{-1}  \\
\end{array}
$$

The microterran subscale preserves the same logarithmic spacing as the full terran sequence but simplifies relative comparisons for sub-planetary bodies — e.g., moons, asteroids, or micro-monons — within a single stellar system.

It improves readability and narrative clarity for small-mass bodies such as **Vesta**, **Miranda**, and **Ceres**.

- It is especially useful for monons with masses between $10^{-6}\,t$ and $10^{-1}\,t$, where decimal terran mass values become visually dense or cognitively opaque.  
- This symbolic shorthand allows you to say:
	- “Ceres is about $157$ **microterrans** ($157\,μt$)”
		- instead of “Ceres is $0.000157\,t$.”  

> **In short:** The $μt$ unit supports clarity without sacrificing scalar precision — a vital aid to both symbolic modeling storytelling.  While most useful in describing small-mass duromons, these subscale prefixes can be used wherever a scaling prefix is needed.

## Applications and Examples

$$
\begin{array}{lrll}
\textbf{Example Monon} & \textbf{Mass ($t$)} & \textbf{Symbolic} & \text{Domain}\\
\hline\hline
\text{Miranda} & 0.0000076 & 7.60\text{ μt} & \text{microterran}\\
\text{Ceres} & 0.000157 & 1.57 \text{\;dmt} & \text{demiterran}\\
\text{Luna} & 0.0123 & 1.23 \text{\;ct} & \text{centiterran}\\
\text{Proxima b} & 1.27	& 1.27\text{ t} & \text{terran}\\
\text{Jupiter} & 317.8 & 3.18\text{\;Ht} & \text{hectoterran}
\end{array}
$$


These examples illustrate how WMB notation maintains both intuitive scale and quantitative accuracy across ten orders of magnitude.

Note that the Ontic mass intervals are *descriptive*, not definitive, in WMB: they are part of what describes an object, but they do not define the classification of that object.  What makes fusomons different from duromons is more than their larger mass interval.

[[Monons, Cules, Lexes, and Gates]]

# The Resultant

A **resultant** is the end result of any mathematical operation, especially
when the specific traditional term (e.g., quotient, product, difference,
remainder) is unknown, irrelevant, would distract from the point being made —
or simply doesn't exist.

Use the standard term when it is clear and helpful; otherwise, “resultant”
serves as a general-purpose placeholder that keeps the narrative moving and
avoids unnecessary terminology detours.

***Examples***:
- **Numeric**:
	- “The **resultant** is $3.1416 \longleftarrow 3.1415(9)$.”
	- "The **resultant** is $1.618 \longleftarrow 1.618'03399$."
- **General**:
	- “Divide by the mass and use that **resultant** in the next step.”



🔝 [[Wimby Numerics]]

title: 🔑 The SANC Charter
summary: Defines the four operational forms of SANC — Science-Adjacent, Notationally Clear, and Self-Adjusting — the core methodological philosophy of WCB.
tags:
date: 2025-11-13
status: canonical
version: 1
related:
  - Integrity in Worldmaking — Mythos and Logos
  - GEWE Corollary
---
> [!axiom] **Principle of Practical Empowerment**  
> The purpose of WMB is not to make worlds *correct*, but to make them *coherent.*  
> To that end, every law within it obeys the rule of **SANC** —  
> *Science-Adjacent, Notationally Clear.*

---
## ⚙️ The First Form — **S**cience-**A**djacent, **N**o **C**alculus

WMB operates *adjacent* to real science: close enough for rigor, far enough for freedom.

To that end:

- Uses **algebra, geometry, and trigonometry** — the practical tools any builder can wield.  
- **Not used:** calculus, tensors, or full astrophysics — those live outside our scope.

> [!axiom] **Rigor without rigidity.**  
> Enough math to make worlds believable, never so much that it strangles imagination.

---
## 🧮 The Second Form — **S**traightforward, **A**ppropriate, **N**otationally **C**lear

- **Straightforward:** Every concept is paired with a usable, illustrated example.  
- **Approximate:** When precision matters, we keep it; when approximation suffices, we mark it — explicitly.  
  - This leads to a companion rule:  
    [[The GEWE Corollary]] — **Good Enough for Worldmaking Efforts**  
    > Think of **GEWE** as the ***WMB GUI*** 😉 — the friendly interface between rigor and play.  
- **Notationally Clear:** Symbols remain consistent, legible, and reusable across all modules.

> [!axiom] **Clarity sustains coherence.**  
> The clearer the notation, the stronger the connection between idea and implementation.

---
## 🧩 The Third Form — **S**ufficient **A**nd **N**ecessary **C**onstructs

At its core, WMB builds **models only as complex as they need to be** to serve a worldmaking goal — *and no more.*  
Every equation, taxonomy, or symbolic rule must pass two tests:

| **Criterion** | **Question** | **Purpose** |
|----------------|--------------|--------------|
| **Necessity** | Does it capture a relationship that materially affects the behavior or believability of the cosmos? | Keeps the model honest. |
| **Sufficiency** | Is it simple, accessible, and practical while preserving the needed clarity and accuracy? | Keeps the model usable. |

> [!axiom] **Build no clockwork where a sundial will do.**

**Cross-References:**  
`Integrity in Worldmaking — Mythos and Logos`  |  `GEWE Corollary`  |  `Consistency and Continuity`

---

## 🧭 The Fourth Form — **S**elf-**A**djusting, **N**on-**C**oercive

Worlds are living systems; their models should breathe.

- **Self-Adjusting:** Whenever possible, WMB formulas and frameworks are built to rebalance themselves when conditions change.  
  - If a model breaks under a new parameter, that’s a *signal*, not a failure.  
  - Builders should refine the boundary, not blame the equation.

- **Non-Coercive:** No rule in WMB is absolute.  
  - A law that cannot flex is not a law of *making*; it’s a law of *limitation.*  
  - Every construct is an invitation to experiment — a guideline, not a guardrail.

> [!axiom] **Coherence, not control.**  
> A worldwright’s task is to build systems that sustain themselves, not obey their maker.

---

> [!summary] **The Fourfold Law of SANC**  
> 1. Science-Adjacent, No Calculus → *Rigor without rigidity.*  
> 2. Straightforward, Appropriate, Notationally Clear → *Clarity sustains coherence.*  
> 3. Sufficient And Necessary Constructs → *Build no clockwork where a sundial will do.*  
> 4. Self-Adjusting, Non-Coercive → *Coherence, not control.*

[[The GEWE Corollary]]
[[To GEWE or Not To GEWE]]


🔝 [[01. What is WMB?]]

# The Shortcomings of the Traditional Spectral Type System

> [!info] Special Note
> This article does not propose a new astrophysical truth, but a new way of coordinating existing truths for worldbuilding use.

The classic stellar spectral sequence — O, B, A, F, G, K, M — originated as a cataloging system based on observed absorption lines in starlight. It was never designed to be linear, complete, or even particularly rational. And it shows.

> 🔍 *Spectral types weren’t designed — they accreted.*

- Early classifiers like [**Annie Jump Cannon**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annie_Jump_Cannon) grouped stars by hydrogen line strength.
- Later work (e.g., by Morgan, Keenan, and especially [**Cecilia Payne-Gaposchkin**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cecilia_Payne-Gaposchkin)) retrofitted those types to [[Stellamon Equations of State#Why "Blackbody" Matters Here|surface temperature]].
- The result? **Inconsistent temperature spans**, irregular gaps between classes, and no underlying mathematical symmetry.

![[ST_Temp_Diff_Graph.png]]

## Comparison of Temperature Scales

In the above graph, the filled columns represent the traditional temperatures assigned per spectral type (all are show, but not all are labeled on the horizontal axis).

The orange line represents the linear flow of the Wimby spectral type temperature scale.  Different spectral types have different slopes, but the overall scale is continuous.

A few features are immediately obvious:
- WMB temps are lower than traditional temps for O-class stars
- WMB tends to over report temps for B- and A-class stars compared to the traditional system
- F- through M-class stars show much less variation (see below), with WMB under-reporting in the low G- and high K-class ranges.

## The Solar Correlates

![[ST_Temp_Diff_Graph_F-K.png]]

This graph focuses on spectral types **F0 — K9**, the regime most relevant to Solar Analogs, Cognates, and Twins (**F2 — K9**).  For readability, the lower temperature axis has been truncated at $3500$ K; high-temperature values are shown in full.

Notice:
- WMB slightly over-reports temperatures for early F-class stars.    
- Both data sets converge closely at **spectral type G2** (solar).    
- WMB reports progressively lower temperatures than traditional values from **G3 through K9**.

Because the vertical scale is compressed, the visual divergence between curves appears larger than the underlying numerical differences. In absolute terms:

- The **minimum difference** is **20 K** at spectral type G3 (WMB: 5700 K; traditional: 5720 K).    
- The **maximum difference** is **400 K** at spectral type K2 (WMB: 4700 K; traditional: 5100 K).    
- The **median absolute difference** is **200 K**, with a mean of **208 K**.    
- The **standard deviation** is **99 K**, with a slight negative skew (−0.204).    

These differences are small relative to the total temperature span of the F — K sequence and fall well within the uncertainty bands implicit in traditional spectral typing. For stellamonic purposes, the benefit of a continuous, internally consistent temperature model outweighs the loss of fine-grained empirical fidelity.

**Note on the G/K Boundary**  
In traditional stellar catalogs, the transition between late G-type and early K-type stars is gradual and inconsistently defined, spanning roughly 5200–5000 K depending on source.

Wimby adopts a continuous, monotonic temperature–luminosity relation that places stars below ~5000 K in the K-type regime. As a result, some stars traditionally classified as late G-type in traditional catalogs will appear as early K-type in WMB.

This reflects a shift in emphasis from spectral labeling to system-level behavior and does not imply a physical disagreement.

## 🧭 Why WMB Does This

Worldmaking Basics adopts a **linearized spectral system** so that:

- Each spectral class is assigned a clean temperature range
- Subclasses divide that range evenly
- The system becomes **interpolatable**, **predictable**, and **symbolically clear**

This makes it easier to:

- Calculate stellar parameters from temperature
- Relate stars to their orbits and Thermozones
- Avoid the awkwardness of stars that fall “between classes”

> ⚖️ *The WMB scale isn’t intended to be astrophysically exact — but it is symbolically coherent and consistently usable.*

> [!note]
> In stellamonics, spectral class is treated as a descriptive label rather than a governing parameter. System behavior is determined primarily by stellar mass, luminosity, and lifetime; spectral types are retained as a translation layer for familiarity and reference.


**Wimby Spectral Convention**

In the traditional spectral system, spectral types are **not defined as a continuous temperature scale**, but as a sequence of **diagnostic spectral regimes**. Each class and subtype corresponds to the appearance or disappearance of specific absorption features, not to evenly spaced temperature intervals.

![[Trad_Temp_Classes.jpg]]

Subtypes (0–9) were introduced to refine classification _within_ a class, but **boundaries between classes are defined by qualitative changes in dominant spectral features**, such as the loss of helium lines or the onset of strong hydrogen absorption. At these boundaries, the spectrum changes character rather than transitioning smoothly.

As a result:

- There is no formal definition for intermediate types like _A0.3_ or _B9.6_    
- A star whose effective temperature lies between two class boundaries is assigned to whichever spectral regime its observed lines most closely resemble    
- The system remains **observationally discrete**, even though stellar temperatures are physically continuous

This reflects the historical purpose of the spectral system: **classification by appearance**, not interpolation by temperature.

For instance, in the traditional system, a star with an effective temperature of **9824 K** has no formally defined spectral type; it lies between the canonical A0 and B9 regimes. 

A professional astronomer would classify the star as **A0 or B9 based on its spectrum**, then calmly report a temperature that appears to fall “between” the nominal ranges — because in the traditional system, that isn’t a problem at all.

Under **Wimby’s spectral convention**, temperature is treated as a continuous parameter, and such a star may be assigned a **fractional spectral type** based on its position within the defined temperature span of a class boundary.

Thus: 

- 9824 K lies between A0 (9700 K) and B9 (10700 K).    
- It therefore _must_ correspond to a fractional spectral coordinate.

$$
\begin{aligned}
S &= \dfrac{10000 - 9824}{250} \\
&= \dfrac{176}{250} \\[1ex]
S &= \boxed{\mathbf{0.704}}
\end{aligned}
$$

 — A0.704, in fact.

In stellamonics, **stellar temperature** ($\mathit{\Theta}$ or $K$​) is the *primary descriptor* of stellar behavior. Spectral classes and types are treated as **derived labels**, provided for orientation and cross-reference rather than as governing parameters. All system-level calculations proceed directly from temperature, luminosity, and mass, independent of spectral binning.

![[WMB_Temp_Classes.jpg]]

This does not claim greater physical precision (it’s not), but rather provides a **consistent, interpolatable mapping** between temperature and spectral designation for worldmaking purposes.

**Accordingly**, spectral type could be omitted entirely in the WMB framework, with stellar temperature treated as the primary identifier of a stellamon. Spectral types are retained solely for compatibility with real-world astronomical conventions and ease of reference.

![[Combined_Temp_Classes.jpg]]









# Bottom Line

If your fiction or modeling needs the exact, messy mess of real-world classification? 

Use real-world data. 

But if you need a system that supports symbolic structure, habitability logic, and easy computation?

> Mind the gap. 
> Use the line.
